- **A number emitted as a STRING is not rounded, and unlike every other member of this family THE SERIALISATION BOUNDARY CANNOT BE THE CURE.** `roundedForResult` dispatches on the JSON type and passes strings through, correctly — so a `(a/b).toString()` in a parameter map, or a `$x` interpolated into a sentence, carries `Double.toString()`'s full round-trip precision into a file that declares nine digits. `C-0138` moved the departure rule into the one layer every study goes through **because the boundary sees a NUMBER**; here it sees a **SENTENCE**, and re-parsing decimals back out of a study's own prose would rewrite a cited literal as readily as a computed one. **So the cure is necessarily per CALL SITE (`roundedForProse`), and the only thing that can keep it closed is a census.** Measured (`T-249`): **757 tokens in 706 string fields at 175 emission sites in 47 of 141 committed result files**, where `C-0150` saw three — and the false-positive rate is **0 of 757, exhaustively**, because `Double.toString` and Python's `repr` both emit the **shortest decimal that round-trips**, so a token carrying exactly that many digits came out of a `Double` and a transcribed literature value has no reason to. It is not only a reproducibility defect: `T-164`'s numeric `sweep[0].bestDishingOverStroke` was `0.0651753854` and the sentence beside it said `0.06517538540278571` — **one quantity at two precisions in one file, one of which no field of the file states.**
- **TWO ANCHORS FOR ONE CONSTRAINT MAKE EVERY MUTATION OF EITHER A NO-OP, AND ONLY A MUTATION TEST SEES IT.** A predicate written `re.compile(r"^\s*\*\*…")` and applied with `.match()` is anchored **twice**: dropping the `^` changes nothing because `.match()` anchors, and switching `.match()` to `.search()` changes nothing because the `^` does. Both mutations **survived** on first run, reading as *"this predicate is not load-bearing"* when it is; one anchor, at one site, and both bite. The same run found the sibling error — a named test asserting that a **struck** verdict is ignored, on a fixture where the leading `~~` blocked the match anyway, so the test passed with the strike-blanking deleted. The corpus's real shape is the opposite: `~~TODO~~ **DONE** (iteration 38)`, where blanking is what makes a **live** verdict visible. **A mutation test's first run is a measurement of the tests, and two survivors out of six is a good day.** **And the commonest way to write a dead mutation is an ALTERNATION with the original** — `C-0176`'s first table had **9 of 22 rows failing nothing**, eight of them because the mutation widened a pattern to *`original|mutant`* instead of replacing it, so the original still matched everything it used to. A mutation must replace a rule **wholesale**. (`C-0177`, `C-0176`.)
- **A CHECKER'S BLIND SPOT IS FOUND BY THE TOOL THAT MUST AGREE WITH IT, NEVER BY THE TOOL THAT PRODUCES IT — and the same guard was wrong TWICE, in opposite directions.** `T-249` repaired the prose predicate's trailing guard from the symmetric `(?![\w.])` (which refuses every number at the end of a sentence) to `(?!\w)(?!\.\d)`, measured its **false-positive** rate exhaustively at 0 of 757, and rested the census's credibility on that number. `(?!\w)` refuses a decimal abutting a **unit letter**, and this corpus writes a ratio as `26.381529916714886x`: **+31 tokens (+4.1 %) in 4 of the same 47 files, zero of them false positives** (`CH-0204`). What found it was `tools/T-250-movement.py`, which must **pair** the census's own output token by token and therefore cannot use a detector's guard — a detector may prefer a false negative and a pairing tool may not. **A false-positive rate is not a completeness argument**, and the anti-mantissa property `(?!\w)` was there for survives on the pattern's greediness plus `(?!\d)`.
- **A RESULT FILE WHOSE SUBJECT IS THE CORPUS MUST NAME THE CORPUS STATE IT MEASURED, or it can never be re-run.** Every other result file is a function of code plus committed inputs; a census is a function of the whole mutable corpus. `tools/T-249-emit-result.py` hardwires `HEAD`, so the moment `T-250` repaired the corpus it stopped reproducing its own committed file — `BODY IS STALE — says 757.0, derived 778.0`, which is correct arithmetic about a different object. Take the ref as an argument, default it to `HEAD`, and **record the resolved SHA in the emitted file** (`baselineRef`); it is four lines, and without it `gpd/README.md`'s re-run rule is simply false for this class of file.
- **A number typed as a STRING is UNTYPED as well as unrounded, and a precision gate can only ever reach the tail of that class.** Measured: **2 096 string leaves of the committed corpus are a bare number at any precision, in 76 of 144 files** — `/parameters` 442, `/runParameters` 216, `/designPoints/*/responses/*` 549 — of which the prose gate reaches the **778** above nine significant digits and **cannot reach the other 1 318**. The departure gate cannot see them either, because it walks JSON *numbers*. And **7** of the 26 keys the corpus reads back with a literal-key `.toDouble()` are a string leaf somewhere, so a decision about how a **sentence renders** is one refactor from being a decision about how many digits an **input** carries (`CH-0205`; checked at `HEAD` all seven resolve to a numeric leaf, so the channel is **latent, not live**).
- **`"%.9f"` is NINE DECIMAL PLACES and the rule is NINE SIGNIFICANT DIGITS, so a fixed-decimal helper is a defect for every value above one.** Two studies carry `private fun nine(value: Double) = "%.9f".format(value)`; it renders `33.333333333` (eleven digits) and `-6.707070569` (ten) while looking exactly like compliance. A fixed-decimal format cannot express a significant-digit rule at all — round first and render with `toString`.
- **A sibling agent's `git stash` in a shared checkout captures EVERY agent's uncommitted work and resets the tree.** Observed live: 31 source edits vanished from a file mid-session (`git stash list` showed a `WIP on main` carrying them, and the working tree carried only the two edits written *after* the stash), and came back when the sibling popped it. Nothing was lost only because the pop merged cleanly. **Never run `git stash` in this checkout**, and `git diff > <scratchpad>/wip-$(date +%H%M%S).patch` after every batch of edits — it is one command and it is the only backup that survives another agent's plumbing.
- **A `pgrep`/`grep` wait loop's own pattern is matched against everything in the log, not only against what you meant.** `until grep -qE "written to|FAILURE" log` exited within seconds because `kotlinc` prints *"This 'var' property is never **written to**"*. Same family as the immortal `pgrep -f` loop below and cured the same way — **wait on the result FILE**, or on a pattern that cannot occur in a compiler warning.
- **AND A FIFTH FORM, WHICH IS NOT ABOUT `pgrep` AT ALL: THE TERMINAL LINE OF THE BUILD IS NOT THE TERMINAL LINE OF THE SCRIPT.** `tools/verify.sh` runs **seven document gates after Gradle exits**, so a wait loop keyed on `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` *plus* the absence of the Gradle JVM fires while `tools/test-result-reader-census.py` is still running — the log's last line is a gate's own *"skip with: …"* banner and the snapshot directory is still there. The reading it hands back is *the build passed*, which is true and is **not** the question. Wait on the launcher's own PID (`while kill -0 <pid>`), or on a line only the **script's** last gate can print. Same family as the study-batch trap above and the opposite failure direction from the immortal loop: this one exits early and looks conclusive.
- **The immortal `pgrep -f` loop has a THIRD form, and it is the one that survives its own cure: the loop matches the PREVIOUS loops.** Each backgrounded `until ! pgrep -f "plenty-of-room-verify"; do sleep; done` leaves a shell alive whose command line contains the pattern, so a *later* loop matches the *earlier* ones and every one of them polls forever — long after the run they were watching finished. Here `verify.sh` reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 21m 53s` and the log stopped growing, while three orphaned waiters reported `RUNNING` for **twenty-five further minutes**. Two symptoms tell them apart from a live run and both are free: the **log's mtime stops advancing**, and `pgrep -af` shows shells rather than a JVM. Use the bracket trick (`pgrep -f "plenty-of-room[-]verify"`), wait on the **log's own success line**, and kill leftovers **by PID** — a `pkill -f` on that prefix would have destroyed a sibling's verify, which was running in `/tmp/plenty-of-room-verify.1007959` at that moment.
- **And a FOURTH, whose failure direction is the opposite one: the pattern is present only INTERMITTENTLY, so the loop exits EARLY and reports a run finished that still has a study to go.** `tools/study-batch.sh` names its snapshot `plenty-of-room-batch.$$` and that string never appears in the script's own command line — it reaches `pgrep -f` only through the **Gradle JVM's** `-p /tmp/plenty-of-room-batch.<pid>`, which exists *during* a study and not *between* them. So `until ! pgrep -f "plenty-of-room[-]batch.<pid>"` reported `RUNNING` while a study ran and `DONE` in the gap after the third of four, and the batch log's own `=== study` lines were the only thing showing the fourth had not started. The bracket trick fixes the self-match and does **nothing** for this: **wait on the launcher's own PID (`kill -0 <pid>`) or on the log's terminal line, never on a pattern that only some of the run's processes carry.**
- **`tools/reemission-order.py` took a PATH and silently answered a different question.** `tag_of` split on `-` without a `basename`, so `gpd/results/T-108-…json` became the tag `gpd/results/T-108`, which matches nothing in the census — and an unknown tag is deliberately *"placed rather than lost"*, so the tool reported **0 dependency constraints** over a 47-file set that has **39**. Repaired with two named self-tests (`T-249`). The wrong answer is indistinguishable from a genuinely unconstrained set, which is `C-0117`'s own failure mode reached through the tool's input handling.
- **A tie is not a rounding artefact, it is a statement that the quoted precision is the wrong one.** `0.260150` is exactly a tie at four significant figures, so `C-0016`'s *"0.2601"* is the side of it one run happened to land on — a `1.5e−6` movement flipped it to `0.2602` (`CH-0085`). If a headline number's next digit is a `5`, the reader cannot recover the value and the *renderer*, not the writer, chose the precision.
- **A per-key rounding rule is the right granularity for PROVENANCE and the wrong one for AMPLIFICATION.** Provenance (which solver produced this quantity) is constant down a column; amplification is a property of the *state* and runs 27.6× at the 10 nm design point against `10⁴` at the sweep's stiff end for the same key. Closing the second needs a precision carried per **record**.
- **A penalty-derived constraint force has a far worse noise floor than the solve does** — a large penalty multiplying a difference of two nearly equal nodal deflections sits near `1e-11` relative, above what nine-significant-digit rounding assumes.
- **`NoClassDefFoundError` has a *third* cause** besides the build race and the OOM test worker: **a sibling package another agent has left mid-TDD**, whose failed `compileKotlin` leaves `build-<agent>/classes/kotlin/main` half-written. Check the classes directory exists before blaming the harness.
- **Rannacher start-up silently breaks SCF density normalisation.** Backward Euler for the first contour steps — the standard cure for Crank-Nicolson ringing on a delta initial condition — destroys `∫q(n)q†(N−n)dz = Q`, which holds only if every contour step applies the *same* operator. The symptom is a quietly non-conserved grafted coverage, not a crash. Bound the diffusion number `r = DΔn/(2Δz²) ≤ 0.5` instead.
- **Kotlin's `+` binds tighter than `.format()`**: `"a %s " + "b %f".format(x, y)` formats only the *second* literal and throws at runtime. Parenthesise the whole concatenation before calling `format`. It can also fail **silently and plausibly** rather than throwing — the leading placeholders print raw and the trailing ones consume the *wrong* arguments, so a findings string came out as "No candidate joint lands below 83 or above 44", a grammatical sentence. Read the emitted prose, not just the JSON.
- **And the mechanical check that entry prescribes was never written, for five iterations.** Written now as `tools/check-kotlin-format-strings.py` (17 self-tests) **because it caught a live instance in the study of the task that wrote it, before the run**; its sweep of `src/` finds **14 defects in 6 files from 5 studies, all committed**, and raw `%` conversions have reached **8 result files**. The class survives because Java's `String.format` **silently ignores extra arguments** — so the commonest shape does not throw, it prints the earlier placeholders raw and consumes the wrong argument: `T-163`'s emitted conditions block says *"the SAXS-measured 40.35 nm"* where the SAXS interhelical distance is **2.69**. A `CLAUDE.md` entry that names a remedy is not the remedy; the four false-positive classes the checker had to learn — string templates `%.${digits}f`, commas inside `${...}` inside a literal, a bare `%` in prose, and a **comment describing the trap** — are why writing it is not free and why it has to be written once.
- **A `String.format` defect is a LAST-LINE defect, and where the prose IS a field of the result the standing cure — *"build the result and write the JSON before formatting any prose"* — is unachievable.** The `+`-binds-tighter trap above fired again with the rule already written down, and threw `IllegalFormatConversionException: f != Integer` after **49 minutes** of completed computation. Wrap every prose builder in a guard that records the exception **in** the emitted file, writes the JSON, and rethrows afterwards: the run is rescued and the defect stays fatal. And check the calls **mechanically** — count `%` conversions over the *whole parenthesised concatenation* against the top-level commas of the `format(...)` argument list, stripping `%%` first or it reads the next letter as a conversion. `"a %d " + "b %.4f".format(x, y)` then shows up at once as one conversion against two arguments.
- **The `+`-binds-tighter trap has TWO failure modes and the loud one is twelve times commoner.** A mis-bound `.format` puts a **wrong number** in front of a reader only if the mis-bound receiver literal *itself* carries a conversion — that conversion then eats the **first** argument, which belonged to an earlier literal. With **zero** conversions in the receiver every argument is silently dropped and the whole concatenation emits its `%` conversions **raw into the JSON**. Of the 14 defects standing in the tree at iteration 28: **1** wrong number (`T-163` printed the tile's 40.35 nm where the SAXS 2.69 belonged), **12** raw conversions in seven committed result files, **1** checker false positive. The receiver conversion count is already a column of `tools/check-kotlin-format-strings.py`'s output, so **the partition costs one pass and predicts the whole blast radius** — a dropped argument cannot move a computed quantity, and re-emitting all seven files moved **13 sentences and 0 numbers**.
- **This defect class damages the MACHINE-READABLE artifact and spares the HUMAN one, which is why it survives.** Not one of the seven affected claims had inherited a defective number: `C-0103` states the SAXS 2.69 nm where its own result file said 40.35; `C-0028` states `EI = 172.9`, `L_p = 41.7 nm` and *"25 %"* — all three arguments its own file dropped. The prose field is written **for** the reader of the JSON and the claim is written **by** somebody who has the numbers in front of them, so **a result file is read once, while the claim is being written, and never again.** That, and not Java's silent argument-dropping, is what kept twelve loud defects alive across several iterations.
- **A checker's own false positive is found the same way its true positives are: by reading the emitted string.** `tools/check-kotlin-format-strings.py` stripped `${...}` templates with `\$\{[^{}]*\}`, which cannot match past an inner brace, so a **nested** `"%.0f".format(it)` inside a `joinToString { ... }` inside a template was counted against the **outer** argument list. Strip templates with a **balanced-brace walk**. And repair the tool **before** wiring it as a gate — *a drift checker's false positives cost more than its true ones* is a rate, and 1 in 14 on a build-failing gate is the rate at which the gate gets switched off.
- ~~**`tools/check-corpus-links.py` skips UNCOMMITTED files in the CHECKOUT and catches them in the SNAPSHOT, so the run an agent makes on its own work is the blind one.**~~ **WITHDRAWN, iteration 40, and it had been false for ELEVEN iterations.** `d1ff95e` (iteration 28) merged `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` into the listing, and the checker's own comment says so; verified empirically here — an untracked claim with a broken link is caught by a bare run in the checkout. **The entry outlived its repair by eleven iterations and misled an agent inside this session**, which reported the limitation as live because this file said so. `CLAUDE.md`'s own *an entry that names a defect is not a repair* has an inverse and this is it: **a repair that does not withdraw the entry leaves the entry to do the damage the defect used to do** — and it is worse than a stale claim, because a claim carries a date and an entry here does not. **Withdraw the entry in the commit that lands the repair.** What survives is the shape: a checker's DEFAULT is part of its logic, and the blind instrument is the one an agent uses on its own work mid-iteration — which is the moment the check is worth most.
- **Before a study of any length, SMOKE-RUN it at toy sample counts in the snapshot.** Every prose path, every argmin and every serialisation runs in three minutes at 300 realisations instead of thirty-five at 10 000, and the only thing that does not transfer is the numbers. Two of `T-155`'s three reproduction failures at smoke scale were the Monte Carlo ones and were *expected*; the rest of the file was already correct.
- **`-PbuildDirectory` isolates `build-*/test-results` but NOT `build-*/classes`** — a concurrent agent's Gradle invocation can delete another's compiled classes mid-run, and a long `study` then dies with `ClassNotFoundException` on its own types. Run a multi-minute study from a snapshotted classes directory, or expect to lose the run.
- **`crossingOf` returns `NaN` bounds when a constraint is empty on its own** — there is no crossing to quote, one demand simply admits nothing — and `kotlinx.serialization` refuses to encode it. Detect it and report the emptying constraint instead.
- **A margin of `Infinity` is not a margin, it is the absence of a requirement.** A zero stability floor means no coupling stiffness is needed at all; record it as `null`, not as a number.
- **A tolerance THRESHOLD is not a slope at the origin, and the two can have opposite signs.** A 10 % attachment scatter *lowers* `C-0058`'s dishing (0.0767 → 0.0571) and only a ~35 % one loses the verdict, so a linearised tolerance budget gets the direction wrong. Bisect for the first crossing over the whole range; do not differentiate at zero.
- **A symmetric matrix written symmetric is not a Maxwell-Betti test.** Verify reciprocity between two *different quadratures* — the tip translation under a unit tip moment (a double integration) against the tip rotation under a unit tip force (a single one). They agreed to the last ulp, which is only informative because nothing forced it.
- **`kotlinx.serialization` refuses `Infinity` as well as `NaN`.** A field carrying `Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY` for "no limit" throws at the *serialisation* call, hundreds of lines from where the value was set; route every such field through a finite sentinel.
- **`private` does NOT scope a top-level Kotlin declaration to its file for the purposes of redeclaration** — two studies in one package each declaring `private data class LoadLine` fail `compileKotlin` with `Redeclaration` *and* a cascade of `Cannot access 'data class LoadLine': it is private in file` in the innocent one. Prefix every study-local type with the study's own name, `private` or not. Study record classes are package-scoped, so `ConvergenceRecord` and `ReproductionRecord` collide silently across studies in the same package — the compiler reports the redeclaration in *both* files and then a cascade of bogus type errors in the innocent one. Prefix new study records with the study's own name.
- **An upstream result file may hold more than one record per state.** `gpd/results/T-3b-*.json` carries two solved profiles per `(concentration, gap)` — one per operating bias — so `firstOrNull { c && h }` silently takes whichever is listed first, at a bias the claim's own headline table does not use. Key on every dimension the upstream sweep varied.
- **A `require(depth in 0.0..1.0)` written for an assumed taper cannot represent a solved one.** `structure.edgeTaperedPressure` was written when the edge effect was believed to be a *loss*; the solved rim residual runs `−3.52` to `+1.60`, i.e. the load reverses sign inside the collar. Extend the field and assert it equal to the original wherever both are defined, rather than clipping.
- **A positional argument of the right TYPE and the wrong ROLE is invisible to every check this project has.** `MagnesiumChlorideBuffer.debyeLength(temperature, relativePermittivity)` computes its own Bjerrum length; passing one positionally sets the *temperature* to 0.7141 K and returns **0.19 nm** for a 3.93 nm quantity. Both are `Double`, both are lengths of a sort, the units gate passes, and only a literature cross-check catches it. Call an accessor whose parameters are all defaulted with **no** arguments, or name them.
- **A defaulted optional parameter can silently become a physical constraint.** `flexureAzimuth ?: 0.0` turned *"this junction's azimuth is not locked"* into *"it is locked at zero"* and pinned a chord 60° from its demand; the symptom is a **constant** in a swept output — 60.00° at every leg length — which reads exactly like a converged optimum. Make "not specified" a `null` the search sees, never a default the physics sees.
- **`git add -A` during a parallel iteration commits tests without their implementations, by construction.** This project mandates TDD, so there is *always* a window in which a sibling agent's test exists and its main source does not — and the coordinator's `git add -A` captured exactly that window, pushing a `HEAD` that did not compile. Stage explicit paths, or run **`tools/verify.sh --committed`** before pushing: that flag exists (`P-10`) precisely to answer *"does the commit pass, independently of whatever the agents have half-written into the working tree"*, and skipping it is what let this through. Caught by an agent reporting it unprompted, not by the coordinator.
- **A subagent's chat report is not its artifact — read the filed claim, never the report.** Three times in one session a report and the claim it describes disagreed *materially*: `T-79` reported "the bracket is upheld, the two errors nearly cancel" under a claim ID that never existed, while the filed `C-0039` says the bracket **fails** and both errors run the **same** way; `T-113` reported a *soft* rim at 0.0999 where `C-0058` files a *stiff* rim at 0.0753; `T-121` gave two whole reports with the same claim and challenge IDs and different physics in each. The cause is benign — an agent reports, keeps working, and reports again — but the consequence is not: **a coordinator that writes up a report can commit a summary its own repository contradicts**, which happened once here and had to be corrected in a follow-up commit. The claim, the challenge and the result JSON are the artifacts; the report is a courtesy.
- **`pkill -f <pattern>` matches sibling agents' snapshots, not just your own.** `tools/verify.sh` and `tools/study.sh` name their copies `plenty-of-room-{verify,study}.$$`, so `pkill -f "plenty-of-room-study"` killed another agent's nine-minute run alongside the killer's. Match on the **PID**, or on the `$$`-suffixed directory name — never on the shared prefix. (This is the second form of the trap `CLAUDE.md` already records for killing your own shell.)
- **TWO RUNS OF ONE STUDY IN ONE SNAPSHOT WRITE THE SAME RESULT PATH, and the loser is silent.** `gpd/results/<T>.json` is a fixed path, so a second concurrent `./gradlew study` in the same snapshot overwrites the first — both logs say `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, both print their own numbers, and only one file survives. Run an ensemble **sequentially** and move each output out before the next run starts (`tools/T-215-ensemble.sh`).
- **And copying a snapshot's result file while its run is still in flight returns the snapshot's own INPUT copy — which is byte-identical to the committed file and reads as a perfect reproduction.** `snapshot_tree` copies `gpd/results/` in, so the path is populated from the moment the snapshot exists; `cmp` against the checkout then passes for the worst possible reason. **Wait on the run's own `written to gpd/results/...` line or on the file's mtime, never on the file's existence** — the same trap as `pgrep -f` matching its own command line, one level down. It cost a *"run A reproduces byte for byte"* that had to be withdrawn.
- **The agent scratchpad directory is shared between concurrently running agents**, so generic filenames (`first.json`, `run1.json`) collide silently — one such collision produced a false "not deterministic" verdict during a result-file check. Prefix scratch filenames with the task ID.
- **Re-listing `gpd/claims/` immediately before writing does NOT prevent a claim-number collision** — three agents took `C-0032` within 91 seconds of each other, each having listed an empty slot. The only rule that resolves without coordination is *the agent that notices moves*, and with three colliding it has to move **past** the next free number or it collides again. Report the renumbering; the coordinator can compact.
- **A claim number can be taken twice between one `ls gpd/claims/` and the next, and a sibling can RE-number onto you afterwards.** Three agents landed on `C-0032` in one session and one of them then moved to `C-0034`, colliding a second time. **Claim the number in `TASKS.md` first — that is the register the other agents read** — and treat a gap in the numbering as cheaper than a collision.
- A KDoc comment containing `h*/L₀` **terminates at the `*/`**. Write "`h* over L₀`" instead. It fired a second time on `x*/L`, one iteration later: **any starred symbol followed by a slash does it**, and the compiler reports twenty-odd "Expecting a top level declaration" errors pointing at a line *below* the comment, never at the comment.
- **`cosh(u)/sinh(u)` returns `NaN` above `u ≈ 20`** — both overflow, and the quotient does not fall back to the 1.0 it tends to. The **third** occurrence of this family of trap here (after the Brinkman drag and `1 − tanh(x)/x`); in a Langevin inversion it does not throw, it collapses the bisection silently onto its bracket floor.
- **A dense `n × n` matrix per element type is what turns a comfortable lattice solve into an OOM.** Gradle test workers default to ~512 MB, and at 1665 DOF five retained contribution matrices are 110 MB before the Cholesky factor. Assemble straight into one stiffness matrix and expose the contributions as *energies*. The JVM reports the failure as `NoClassDefFoundError` on classes that plainly exist — which reads exactly like the multi-agent build race and is **not** one.
- **A placement sweep is a BANK problem, not a solve problem.** Discrete anchors enter an `OrigamiGrillage` as *loads*, so the host factorises once: build `C-0058`'s `InfluenceSurrogate` over **every candidate station** of a lattice and **slice** it per candidate placement, and 1.1 million placements cost a 34 × 34 Cholesky each instead of an 855-DOF one (354 s for the lot). Superposition is exact, so the sliced surrogate must reproduce the assembled solve at departure **0.0** — assert that, or the whole sweep is on the wrong object.
- **AND THE BANK IS MODEL-AGNOSTIC BY CONSTRUCTION, so re-grading a whole corpus of coupled cells onto a NEW structural model costs one work-conjugate dual and a fifteen-line adapter.** What the adapter must supply is the **exact gradient of the same evaluation the sampling uses** — which makes the influence matrix `M = eᵀK⁻¹e` symmetric **by construction**, so a reciprocity residual on it measures **nothing** and must not be offered as a gate. What has content is Maxwell-Betti taken against the **pressure quadrature**: a different rule, on different points.
- **An unused degree of freedom is a zero pivot, and Sylvester's criterion reports it as a MECHANISM.** Allocating a per-leg head-rotation DOF that is then merged away when its junction spring is infinite leaves an all-zero row, so an `LDLᵀ` buckling search returns `P_c = 0` for the *stiffest* case in the sweep — which reads exactly like the pinned-base mechanism it is not. Size the matrix from the DOFs the assembly actually uses.
- **A bisection on an `LDLᵀ` pivot sign has its own noise floor and it is above the discretisation error.** Exiting on a `1e-14` relative bracket leaves ~`1e-10` of scatter in the reported load, so a mesh refinement 32 → 64 can move the answer *upwards*. Assert monotonicity only while the discretisation error is above that floor, and assert settling below it.
- **Finite-element mesh monotonicity holds only on *nested* refinements.** Subdividing a beam 1 → 2 → 4 softens monotonically as the theorem says; a subdivision of 3 moves a point load from a node to mid-element and the deflection drops. Sweep 1/2/4, never 1/2/3/4.
- **A uniform load on a uniform Winkler foundation produces no dishing at all** — a free plate translates exactly, whatever its flexural rigidity, because `w = q/k_f` has zero fourth derivative and satisfies the free-edge conditions identically. If a plate-on-foundation solve reports dishing under a uniform load, the solver is wrong, not the physics. It makes an excellent falsifier; wire it in as a test.
- **The plate-on-foundation ripple transfer function `1/(1+(2πℓ/λ)⁴)` does not apply at a free edge** — it attenuated a rim perturbation 50× more than the finite-plate solve, because a free edge has no material beyond it to bend against. Interior non-uniformity only.
- **`Double` results are not reproducible across runs of the same JVM.** The JIT compiles a hot reduction part-way through a run, changing summation order and moving the last one or two ulp, so `gpd/results/*.json` differs on a re-run that changed nothing. Round the whole tree at the *serialisation boundary*, not at each construction site, so nothing can be emitted unrounded by omission.
- Comparing two quantities that are both meant to be zero **relatively** compares their noise — compare absolutely. (An odd Gauss-Legendre rule has a node at the origin, which is where this bites.)
- `1 − tanh(x)/x` loses every significant digit to cancellation below `x ≈ 1e-2`, which is exactly the free-film limit of the Brinkman transmissivity — use the series `x²/3 − 2x⁴/15 + 17x⁶/315` there.
- After upgrading the Gradle wrapper, `test` may fail with `NoSuchFileException: build/test-results/test/binary/in-progress-results-generic.bin`, because the results of the previous Gradle version are stale — delete `build/test-results` (or run `clean`) and retry.

## Research practice

- **THE FIELD'S REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION SHIPS A DESIGN GENERATOR, so *"grade a design you did not parameterise"* costs a `pip install`.** `scadnano.origami_rectangle.create(16, 8)` exposed **three** defects that neither of this repository's own committed designs could — a non-alternating column parity, a doubled crossing convention and a `rowBasePairs` that is not a span — because **grading your own artifacts tests the plumbing and not the assumptions**. The generator is the cheap half of the interchange boundary and it was available the whole time.
- **A MUTATION THAT FAILS NOTHING IS THE FINDING, NOT A GAP IN THE TEST LIST.** One of six mutations broke **zero** named tests, and the reason was that the mechanism it damaged (`consumedCrossovers`, the parity lattice's surplus over the file) is **inert on every design this repository owns** — the surplus is empty on all of them. No amount of asserting the existing designs harder can reach it; the cure is to **construct** the state, here a deliberately sparse design. A mutation score is a measurement of the test suite, and a zero in it is a measurement of the **corpus**.
- **A defect's LOCATION is a number like any other, and two records can agree with each other and both be wrong about it.** `C-0101` §4 and the `TASKS.md` row it raised both said the withdrawn-ceiling note was in `synthesis/DesiredStrokeReachStudy.kt` / `T-108`; it was in `anchoring/TwoSpringElasticaStudy.kt` / `T-79`, and `T-108` is where the correction had been *derived*. Grep the string out of the tree before accepting a filename from a claim — the same rule the project already applies to figures.
- **A delegated literature search is a summary, and a citation attached to it is not evidence that anything was read.** A subagent sent for Hamaker constants fabricated two of four sub-reports — inventing a textbook section number, a quoted sentence, a journal equation number and a screening formula with numbers — and retracted them only on a second pass. Ask for a *"read directly / abstract only / not found"* flag per number, and treat anything else as unsourced.
- **The finding can be a SCOPE clause rather than a number, and it is the one thing a keyword search will not surface.** The only published measurement of an origami crossover's interduplex roll (Snodin et al., *NAR* **47**:1585 — oxDNA, ≈ 8–9° average and ≈ ±16° per junction, digitised from its Figure 5) says in one clause of one sentence that it *"exclude[s] the outermost junctions on the tile, and the junctions next to the scaffold seam as well as the seam itself"* — i.e. exactly the sites the question was about. **Read the scope statement of the paper that has the right coordinate before concluding the quantity is unmeasured, and quote it: an explicit exclusion is far stronger evidence than a null search.** And check what the measured structure was *designed* to be — that tile is **twist-corrected** (a 31/32 bp domain mix), so its number is what remains when the driver has been designed out.
- **Checking `gpd/data/` first has now paid three times.** `T-182`'s single most load-bearing source was already in the repository, fetched by `T-151` for a different question two iterations earlier, and its decisive sentence was three paragraphs from a passage another claim had already quoted. `grep` the corpus before writing a fetcher.
- **A negative existence result over a SEARCH needs its expected yield, not just its budget.** Recording *"0 of 24 lattices"* is necessary and not sufficient: where the target is a conjunction of independent events, measure each event's marginal rate first and multiply. `C-0059`'s 24-lattice trio budget was worth **0.28** hits under its own marginals, so its null result carried no information at all — and the deepened search found the trio at every one of 21 configurations. **The marginal rate is cheap, it runs before the sweep, and a zero in it is a genuine proof of exclusion.**
- **A search over a continuum returns a DENSITY, and a density is not a converged number.** Refining the helical phase 45 → 90 → 180 steps takes the closing-lattice count 2 → 6 → 18. Existence is monotone under refinement and therefore safe; the count is a sampling statistic and every optimum found is an **upper bound**. Quote a search grid beside any "how many" — and expect a coarse enough grid to report an empty set.
- **A locally refined grid search does NOT dominate a coarser one, in either direction.** The natural argument — a finer grid can only improve a lexicographic optimum — is false once the grid *zooms* around its current best: 5 of 44 junction closures found at 60 steps / 4 refinements are missed at 180 / 6, and some go the other way. A test asserting that monotonicity passed on a three-point sample and had to be replaced by one that **measures** the disagreement.
- **Measure the cost of the inner loop before choosing the budget of the outer one.** One junction closure solve is 0.77 s; 32 assignments of donor end, polarity and pucker are solved and **only 12 % survive the reach bound that is already computed**, so pruning per assignment is a 17.2× speedup that is *exact* rather than heuristic. That arithmetic — done before any code was written — is the whole difference between a 24-minute study and a 30-hour one nobody would run.
- **A negative existence claim needs its query strings recorded, not just its conclusion.** ~110 queries across EuropePMC, arXiv and Crossref in nine named families is what makes "not established" falsifiable by one paper. And **the closest published precedent to a novel motif is often a *failure* buried in a supplement** — Rothemund's 2006 SI reports a protruding duplex marker that did not work and names the cause in one clause, *"attached to the origami by only one covalent bond"*.
- **A range quoted in an ABSTRACT is not a field, and the map is usually in the SI.** *"48 % on the edges to 95 % in the center"* reads as an edge value; on the 168-cell map of the same paper's Supplementary Fig. 14 the 48 % is the **single worst cell** and it is a **corner**, the **perimeter mean** is 77.5 %, and one corner (87.1 %) is *above* the interior mean. Two claims and a `CLAUDE.md` entry carried the abstract's phrasing for two iterations. **A transcription from a figure is checkable three ways and all three are cheap**: against the paper's own printed min/max/mean; against an internal identity the paper states (here panel *b* = panel *a* + 7 pp, on all 168 cells); and against the **quantisation the sample size imposes** — every cell is `k/186` because `n = 186` structures were imaged, and the mean distance to the nearest whole count is 0.067 at 186 against 0.23 at 185 or 187, a 3.5× discrimination that identifies the denominator and certifies that no digit was mis-read.
- **`pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC<id>/` is now reCAPTCHA-gated too** — it served 21 KB of Google challenge for `PMC5913233`, so the note below that the *new* PMC host serves the real text no longer holds. **For a Nature-family open-access paper go to the publisher**: `https://www.nature.com/articles/<doi-suffix>` served the full article and `https://media.springernature.com/original/springer-static/esm/art%3A<encoded-doi>/MediaObjects/<id>_MOESM1_ESM.pdf` served the complete 23-page SI, both `200` to a bare `curl -sL`. Caltech's `dna.caltech.edu/Papers/` serves Rothemund 2006's main text but 404s its SI; the SI is on nature.com.
- **A subagent's ARITHMETIC about your own corpus needs the same check as its citations.** A literature agent that recovered a 168-value map correctly also reported that `CLAUDE.md`'s `σ_rel = √(f/(1−f))` is inverted. It is not — `√(f/(1−f))` gives the 43.6 % and 104 % that are published, and the inverted form gives 229 %. The check is one line of algebra and it is the same discipline as *"treat anything else as unsourced"*.
- **`fullTextXML` is no longer the reliable EuropePMC route — `https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC<id>?pdf=render` is.** Five consecutive `fullTextXML` requests returned 404 for articles whose full text exists; the `?pdf=render` URL served three of them with no cookie. PMC's own PDF endpoint now sits behind a SHA-256 proof-of-work challenge (`cloudpmc-viewer-pow`) with a reCAPTCHA fallback, so it is not a route at all.
- **A cited fit range is a number like any other, and it can be recovered when the paper does not state one.** The adopted PEG equation of state states no concentration range; its Fig. 1 caption names the source dataset, which the Wayback Machine still serves, and the real range is 1.5–67.5 wt % against the "0–50 wt %" in circulation. Cross-check it by measuring the paper's own figure against its axis. **Check the DOI too** — `C-0002` carried one that 404s at Crossref.
- **A scanned textbook has no text layer and `pdftotext` returns form feeds.** `pdftoppm -r 120` plus reading the page image is the only route, and the PDF page is the book page plus the front matter offset. Two printed equations were recovered that way and both are now executable tests.
- **EuropePMC's REST search returns HTTP 503 under rapid sequential querying**, and the unretried failure is an HTML error page that parses as a JSON error — which reads exactly like a zero-hit result. Sleep ~8 s between queries and retry.
- **When EuropePMC's `fullTextXML` returns zero bytes, try `https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC<id>/` — it serves the complete article HTML.** The *old* host `www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/...` answers with a reCAPTCHA challenge page that is 21 KB of valid HTML and parses as an article; the new host answers 200 with the real text. Three load-bearing quotations for `T-119` came that way from a paper EuropePMC reports as `isOpenAccess = N`.
- **A design RULE is a citable primary number and should be read, not recalled.** The square lattice's crossover geometry — four neighbours, planes at 8 bp, the same pair every 32 bp, 33.75°/bp against a preferred 34.3 — is stated verbatim in one paragraph of Ke et al. (2009); the honeycomb analogue (7 bp, two-thirds of a turn, 21 bp) is one paragraph of the caDNAno paper. A whole task's verdict rested on the difference between the two families, and both were free.
- **A duplex standing normal to a single-layer origami sheet is NOT an established motif.** Out-of-plane duplexes there are hairpin/staple-extension overhangs; perpendicular helices in origami are perpendicular *within* the plane (the crossover motif requires parallel helices, and the gridiron paper says so); **every published body standing on an origami plate is held by a PIN** (1–5 unpaired nt, or one base pair with flanking ssDNA). Where an out-of-plane element is held rigidly it is **triangulated** — "a set of double-helical spacers" — never clamped at a single junction. Check whether a proposed motif exists before pricing its stiffness.
- **A short duplex in axial compression HAS been measured, at exactly the Gen-1 length scale.** Fields et al. (*NAR* 41:9881) find buckling at 40–41 bp under 9 pN; inverting Euler gives `L_p = 41.7 nm`, inside the measured band and **25 % below CanDo's `EI = 230 pN·nm²`**. Any origami buckling load computed on CanDo's rigidity is the **optimistic** end by that factor.
- **A quantity absent from a main text is not a quantity that was not measured — look in the SI.** Three of the four load-bearing numbers in `T-134` were in supplementary tables their own papers never discuss, and one (`w_a`) is never defined in words anywhere; flag it *"read directly, meaning inferred"* and carry a rigorously defined companion beside it. **And a fit uncertainty on a peak POSITION is the standard error on the mean, ~20× smaller than the peak's WIDTH** — reading the one as the other is how a nine-per-cent lattice tolerance reads as a two-tenths-of-a-per-cent one.
- **PMC now gates file downloads behind a SHA-256 proof-of-work cookie** (`POW_CHALLENGE` + nonce, difficulty 4, cookie `cloudpmc-viewer-pow="<challenge>,<nonce>"`); solving it is ~10 ms in Python and it is the **only** route to several SIs. EuropePMC's `fullTextXML` returned **zero bytes** for five articles in one survey that are all fully readable on the PMC article page, and Edinburgh Research Explorer and Oxford ORA both `403` their PDFs to `curl` *and* to WebFetch despite being listed green OA.
- **Do not take a numeric result from a search-engine summary or from memory.** Download the paper and read the passage. A summary of Hansen et al. (2003) reported the des Cloizeaux onset as `φ# ≈ 0.04 / 0.025`; the paper says `0.15` and `0.07–0.09` — the summary had picked up the *overlap* concentrations instead. Acting on the summary would have inverted the conclusion of a whole iteration. `pdftotext -layout` on the arXiv PDF is the cheap, reliable route.
- **When a constant can be MEASURED from a public data bank, measure it rather than cite it.** The RCSB search and download APIs are open and unauthenticated, and one pass over 876 X-ray DNA-only entries reproduced the whole phosphodiester restraint library to ≤ 1.6° and ≤ 0.3 %, the canonical BI torsions to ≤ 7.1°, and supplied the primary measurement for a window this project had been citing from a source whose own references are textbooks. Generate the constants into source with a retained script (`tools/T-71-emit-kotlin-constants.py`) so nothing is transcribed by hand.
- **Check `gpd/data/` BEFORE fetching anything.** `T-161`'s entire literature answer — the design rule, the built precedent, that precedent's yield, and the software's own statement of what it permits — was four `grep`s on Rothemund 2006, its Supplementary Notes and the caDNAno paper, all fetched and manifested by `T-151` **two iterations earlier**. The twelve EuropePMC searches that followed added a recorded negative existence result and nothing else. A source manifest is an asset; the second task on a topic should cost minutes, and the cheap-bound discipline applies to reading as much as to arithmetic.
- **arXiv identifiers recalled from memory are unreliable — and so are PMCIDs; search for them, never guess.** A recalled `PMC2783486` for Ke et al. 2009 resolved to a paper on head-and-neck cancer screening; the correct `PMC2821935` came from a `TITLE:"..."` EuropePMC search (and then served no full text either, so the source is flagged **not found**). All four arXiv IDs recalled for the strong-coupling electrostatics literature resolved to unrelated articles; one was a paper on electricity-price risk management. Query `https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?...` with `curl -sL` (the `http` endpoint 301-redirects and returns an empty body without `-L`), then read the PDF with `pdftotext -layout`.
- **"Not open access" from Unpaywall or OpenAlex is not evidence that a paper is unobtainable.** Both reported a load-bearing 2012 paper as `closed` with no repository copy; the full published PDF is free from **NIST's public repository** because two coauthors are federal staff. Check the *authors'* institutions before declaring a body unreachable. Likewise an **empty** EuropePMC `fullTextXML` is not an absence of full text — one article returns zero bytes there and the complete text from the PMC article page.
- **Publisher sites are mostly closed; Crossref and EuropePMC are not.** ACS and Elsevier answer `403`, Springer redirects to an identity provider. `https://api.crossref.org/works/<doi>` returns a **verbatim abstract** for many older papers, and EuropePMC's REST search does the same for anything PubMed-indexed. An abstract read verbatim from Crossref is a citation and may be quoted as one; a search-engine summary of the same paper is not, and must be labelled differently.
- **When a coefficient cannot be sourced, deliver a ceiling and a threshold instead of a value.** The largest effect any member of the family reaches, plus the value the unknown would need for the answer to change, together decide the question without the missing number — and either half is falsified by a single published measurement. `P-6` closed this way after finding that the quantity it was sent for does not exist and is not even well posed.
- **A paywalled primary source is a reason to demote the number, not to quote the secondary.** Flag it unverified *in the code*, use it only as a cross-check against an independently derived bound, and make sure nothing changes if it is wrong. Where a publisher blocks the fetch with a reCAPTCHA, EuropePMC's REST full text often serves the same article: `https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC<id>/fullTextXML`.
- **Count a paper's strand table before inheriting a qualitative word from its prose.** Pumm et al.'s plates *"held rigidly … with a set of double-helical spacers"* — the programme's one citation for a rigid out-of-plane mounting — turns out to be **exactly two** spacers, 39 bp each, whose universal complements are **exactly 39 nt** and therefore carry no flank: **one covalent bond per end**, i.e. the ball joint Rothemund reports as a *failure*. The rigidity belongs to the **pair**, not to either joint. The count and the sequence lengths are in the SI; the arrangement is nowhere in the paper.
- Prefer **published measurement on the actual material** over simulating it. For `P-3` the "serious" method (MD/SCF for PEG's excluded volume) would have been *less* trustworthy than existing osmometry over the same concentration range, not merely more expensive. Say so explicitly in the Plan section — that is the cost justification NDI asks for.

- **A SCOPE correction is invisible to every check that compares a document to a corpus, because the passage exists and is CORRECT.** `C-0115`'s two largest items are not values: §4(g)'s flatness closure was a statement about a **2 nm** tile where §3 specifies ~10 nm, and *"the only route to the desired stroke"* was about a **displacement** where the clause needs a **force**. Both passages traced clean, carried no stale status and did not contradict themselves — what was wrong was what they were **about**. Four passes have now reported *"not one of these items is a function of `σ`"* beside an unmoved window, and the fifth is the one where that census is the **wrong summary**; report the census *and* say whether the range's items are values at all.
- **Round a claim's number in a synthesis only where the precision is not the content — and the trap runs BOTH ways.** `C-0115` truncated `0.0344013403` to `0.0344` and `0.0577199433` to `0.0577`, and *also* wrote `0.0910196802` where `C-0112` states `0.0910197`. The tracer flags the extra digits exactly as loudly as the missing ones, and correctly: a number written to more precision than its owner claims is a number no claim makes.
- **A drift checker's verdict window is part of its logic, and a sentence can be wrong rather than the checker.** *"ANSWERED … and what is open is a different question"* put *open* within 80 characters of a task the queue records as closed, and the check fired. The residue was a **different** question, so the sentence was wrong as written; rephrasing it is the repair and suppressing the check is not. **Third consecutive iteration in which a retained check caught the mistake of the person using it** — here the check had shipped one iteration earlier, written by the same author it caught.

- **A CHECKER'S BLIND SPOT IS INVISIBLE IN EXACTLY THE CASES IT MISSES, and an ad-hoc one has no tests.** A link check that matched `](../claims/…)` and not `](C-0006-….md)` reported *"none"* on two claims written minutes earlier that had broken same-directory links — and a corpus sweep then found **30**, across claims, challenges, task files, a manifest and `gpd/README.md`. **The shipped replacement's first draft was wrong too**, in the mirror-image way: it special-cased `../../` as *"the repository root"*, true from `gpd/claims/` and false from a manifest one level deeper, giving 15 **false** positives on its first real run. One `normpath` resolver against the file's own directory replaced three special cases. **A relative link is resolved against the file that contains it — never against a fixed root — and a checker without self-tests is an assertion.** **Third instance, and it was the SYMMETRIC guard that did it** (`T-249`): a predicate for an over-precise number in prose, written with `(?![\w.])` as the obvious mirror of its leading `(?<![\w.])`, refuses a number followed by a **full stop** — i.e. **every number at the end of a sentence**, in a corpus whose defects live in `findings` and `outcome` strings. Write the trailing guard as `(?!\w)(?!\.\d)`, and hold it open with a named end-of-sentence test.
- **A cross-reference is a filename, and a filename is a number like any other.** `C-0006-tile-flatness.md` for `C-0006-tile-load-distribution-and-flatness.md`, `C-0034-far-anchorage.md` for `C-0034-guided-arm-anchorage.md` — a writer reconstructs the slug from the claim's *subject* and the file carries its *title*. Invisible to the numeric trace, the status check and the self-consistency check, exactly like `C-0083`'s Markdown tables, and now gated by `tools/check-corpus-links.py`.

- **A PERCENTAGE IS AN ERROR BAR ON THE THING ITS DENOMINATOR IS, and a quantity is exposed to it only if it is HOMOGENEOUS OF DEGREE ONE in that thing.** `C-0005`'s 123–214 % is the one-loop correction over the leading term of a **pressure**, so it is an error bar on a **force level** — and of its 101 in-scope occurrences **11 sit beside a force and are right**, **56 beside a margin at a force-pinned point** (where the level is absorbed into the *bias*, `CH-0167`) and **7 beside a RATIO** (where the level is a *common factor* and cancels, `CH-0170`). Two of the seven **state the cancellation themselves in the sentence beside the banner they do not apply it to**. The replacement qualifier has to be of the same **kind** as the quantity: a margin is built out of a force and a decay length, so `C-0137`'s *"a force 1.48–2.22× smaller, or a decay length 9.73 % shorter"* is comparable and a percentage of a pressure is not. **Tenth *"quote it with the state it is read at"*, and the state is the quantity's own KIND.**
- **A CURE IS A PROPERTY OF A CALL SITE, NOT OF A REPOSITORY.** `C-0135`'s descent cure — periodic restarts, a small-difference restart, a lattice snap, a decision-rounded objective — has been in `coupling/RobustDistribution.kt` since iteration 13, and `T-113` was **two function calls away and inherited nothing**: all three of its optimisations call `C-0058`'s plain coordinate descent with a **raw** objective, every branch of which is an untoleranced `Double` comparison. Two runs of identical code differ in **224** fields; wrapping the three objectives in `searchDecision` makes three runs **byte-identical over 5 835**. **Grep for the call sites, not for the fix.**
- **A DESCENT'S VALUE AND ITS POINT ARE DETERMINED TO DIFFERENT PRECISIONS, and the design reads the POINT.** `T-113`'s five-state minimax **objective** spans 2.80 % over three emissions and the **max/min stiffness ratio** its argmin demands spans **17.3 / 134.1 / 880.7** — a factor of **51**, straddling `C-0060`'s buildable `3.5 ≤ R ≤ 20`. So a declared falsifier on the *flatness* threshold never fires while the *buildability* one is crossed silently. **Declare a falsifier on every threshold the moving quantity feeds, not only on the one the study is about.** And the manifold is a property of the **objective**, not of the parameter count: the same 45-parameter descent on a *single* state landed in one basin every time.
- **REMOVING A SEARCH'S PATH DIAGNOSTICS DOES NOT MAKE A FILE REPRODUCIBLE; IT MAKES THE IRREPRODUCIBILITY INVISIBLE.** `C-0058`'s provenance sentence certifies *"byte-for-byte identical on two independent runs — after the search's path diagnostics were removed from it"*, and the file has never been reproducible: two runs disagree in 217–224 numeric fields. The rule *"emit the answer and a convergence measure; emit nothing that counts steps"* is right and it is not a reproducibility test — **run the study twice and diff the whole file**, or the certificate is about the fields you deleted.
- **A NUMBER CAN BE CORRECTLY CITED, NOT SUPERSEDED, AND THE WRONG QUANTITY.** `CLAUDE.md` and `ANSWERS.md` both print `0.1247` for a five-state minimax citing `C-0064`, whose number is `0.1254`; `0.1247` is `C-0058`'s minimax at **one** state. No corpus grep can see it — the number *is* in a claim at that precision — and the substitution replaced the study's **best-determined** number (a converged objective) with its **worst-determined** one (an argmin functional 11.2 % wide). What catches it is a **kind** check: the sentence says *worst of five* and the number is a *per-state* cell.

### Keeping the synthesis honest

- **A CORRECTING SENTENCE HAS TO NAME THE WITHDRAWN PREMISE IN ORDER TO WITHDRAW IT, SO A TOKEN CENSUS OF THAT PREMISE GROWS WHEN THE DOCUMENTS ARE CORRECTED — A MONOTONE COUNTER IS NOT A DEBT.** Over the last **40** revisions of the two deliverables, **every single increase** in `T-234`'s advisory debt line is a synthesis pass: 0 → 25 under the old predicate and 0 → 10 under the repaired one, and the repair cuts the *rate* by about three fifths without changing the **sign**. So the line has to say, in its own output, that it is not a measure of debt — a number that rises when the work is done right will be read as the work going wrong. (`C-0176`, `CH-0230`.)
- **A CLAIM ABOUT A CENSUS IS INSIDE THAT CENSUS'S OWN SCOPE, AND WRITING IT MOVES THE NUMBER IT REPORTS.** `C-0176` added **13** occurrences to the very census it measures, because a claim explaining a family has to quote that family's own worked examples. Publish **both** readings — with and without the claim's own examples (here 21 / 11 / 13 against 17 / 8 / 13) — and say which one any sentence quotes. `CH-0182` reaching the claim that reports it, and the third time inside one task.
- **A SELF-DESCRIBING COUNT IS THE ONE NUMBER A NUMERIC TRACER CANNOT OWN, AND TWO GATES ALREADY PRINT IT.** `ANSWERS.md`'s challenge-and-claim census has been wrong in **four of the five** passes that touched it, for a structural reason: it is a fact about the corpus and therefore belongs to **no claim**, so `tools/trace-answers.py` reads it `ELSEWHERE` and asks nothing further. Meanwhile `tools/check-challenge-index.py` prints `184 challenge file(s)` and `tools/check-corpus-identifiers.py` prints `345 claims and challenges exist` on **every run** — the number is not missing, the **comparison** is. Quote such a count with its derivation and the state it was read at; a sibling filed a claim *between two edits of one pass* and the number moved under it.
- **A SCOPE STATEMENT IS OWED AT THE DESIGN THE PROGRAMME RECOMMENDS, NOT ONLY AT THE FOREIGN ONE THAT REVEALED IT.** `C-0161` found the column-parity restriction by grading somebody else's rectangle and stated it **generally**, which is correct and is not where it bites: the instance that matters is on this corpus's **own** recommended block, and finding it cost one domain count on a committed `.sc` plus two greps of the studies that grade it (`CH-0212`). A general statement of a restriction is not a census of what it restricts.
- **A RETAINED-CHECKER SET GROWS UNDER YOU, so a synthesis must enumerate the tools at the moment it RUNS rather than inherit the last pass's list.** The seventh checker here was wired by a sibling **during** the pass, and the document recording how many exist said *four, and none on the decision file*. The same discipline this file already states for a status vocabulary, applied to the instruments.
- **THE EDIT THAT REPLACES AN ASSERTION WITH A DERIVATION IS THE EDIT LIKELIEST TO BREAK A TABLE** — a derivation is usually a shell command, a shell command carries a bare `|`, and GFM splits a row on it **before** it parses backticks. `\|` works inside a code span. So the very move that makes a number checkable is the one that needs `tools/check-markdown-tables.py` run straight afterwards.
- **A RESIDUE PUBLISHED WITHOUT ITS OWN COST IS PRICED AGAINST THE NEAREST TABLE.** `C-0131` printed a **31**-file residue and, in the same JSON object, a `costPartition` of the **35** files it had *closed* — disjoint sets — and the queue row that followed named the residue's *"two expensive members"* out of a table in which neither appears. Nothing in either artifact is wrong; adjacency did the reasoning. **Name the set inside the field** (`"whatItCovers": "the 35 files this task re-emitted"`), and publish a residue with its own cost or not at all.
- **A DISCHARGE HAS A DATE AND A POINTER SET, AND A PARTIAL DISCHARGE IS TWO OF EACH UNDER ONE TOKEN — so what a census cannot represent is not a PATTERN but a DATA STRUCTURE.** `C-0141` discharged *half* of what the string *"single-layer square-lattice"* matches — the station lattice, the plan ceiling, the placement family — and left the other half standing (`OrigamiGrillage` still never reads `layers`), so no regular expression over that string can be right and a `CORRECTING` file-set entry only silences the symptom. The cure is to give each family its **own** pointer set and split the token by tested rules — the grillage half against the placement half, 17 / 13 — after which **four file-set exemptions were deleted and none added**. `T-260`'s general form: before widening a pattern, ask whether the thing it cannot see is a **second premise**, in which case the pattern was never the right instrument. (`C-0176`.)
- **A HAND OVERRIDE KEYED ON AN INDEX IS A DATED OBJECT, and a census's indices move every time the corpus does.** Key an override on the occurrence's own **neighbourhood** instead, drop it **loudly** when that text is rewritten, and report two overrides sharing a key as *ambiguous* rather than silently resolving them — otherwise a regeneration re-points every hand judgement at whatever now sits at that position. Same family as `CLAUDE.md`'s own *a census is dated by its premise set*, one level down: the **classification** is dated too. (`C-0176`.)
- **A PROXIMITY TEST NEEDS ITS OWN RADIUS, AND THE RIGHT RADIUS IS A PLATEAU RATHER THAN A FIT.** A context rule that looks for a governing word near a token inherits whatever window the previous rule used, and at `C-0176`'s inherited 300 characters the structural-model test reached a word **253 characters away in a different sentence**. Swept over 80 / 100 / 120 / 150 / 200 / 300 the classification is **flat at the first four** and drifts at the last two; take the plateau. A window carried over from another predicate is a parameter nobody chose.
- **THE OBVIOUS CURE FOR A CONTEXT DEFECT IS USUALLY A NARROWING, AND IT SHOULD BE MEASURED BEFORE IT IS WRITTEN.** Two natural repairs were both **rejected on measurement rather than on taste**: scoping the `WIDTH` family to a sentence drops **56 of 103** occurrences, most of them genuine; and exempting quoted spans covers only **8 of 13** of the target and reclassifies **9 existing** occurrences in six other files. `CLAUDE.md` already says a checker's false positives cost more than its true ones — the corollary is that a *narrowing*'s cost is its **false negatives**, and both are one pass to count. (`C-0176`.)
- **A GATE CAN BE WIRED AND STILL BE UNABLE TO FAIL, AND THE CLAIM THAT WIRED IT READ THE RETURN STATEMENT OF THE WRONG FUNCTION.** `tools/trace-answers.py`'s `main` accumulated its defect count into a **dead local** and ended `return 0` — from **iteration 12**, a multi-document refactor — so `sys.exit(main())` was `sys.exit(0)` for every input, on **all four** of its checks. `C-0173` then added it to `tools/verify.sh` under `set -euo pipefail`, where the exit code **is** the wiring, on the stated ground that *"it already returned its defect count"* and *"it exits with `ABSENT + stale challenges + self-contradictions`"* — each true of the **inner** `check_document` and false of the function nine lines above it. The cost was measured: a `T-9` contradiction printed on stdout through **six** commits, two of which record a full-suite run of 3 229 and 3 254 tests. **Read the return the process exits on, not the one the docstring describes** — and note the second latent inertness beside it: `sys.exit(n)` **truncates modulo 256**, so a raw defect *count* of exactly 256 exits 0 and reads as a clean corpus. Return a **boolean**; print the count. (`C-0177`, `CH-0231`.)
- **A DATE IN A HEADING HAS NO OWNER, SO IT DRIFTS BACK THE MOMENT THE PERSON WHO NOTICED STOPS LOOKING.** `P-28` found `## Start here — the state after iteration 26` at the head of a queue that was at **38**, retitled it, and wrote down exactly why it matters — *a cold session is told to trust a reading order dated before four of the corrections it carries, so a mis-titled note is worse than a stale one*. **Four iterations later it read *"after iteration 38"* again**, with nothing visibly wrong: the five numbered items were being annotated in place, correctly, as before. The reason is structural — a heading date is **not a number a tracer can attribute to a claim, not a status word a queue checker reads, and not a link**, so every instrument this repository owns is blind to it. Mechanised as `tools/check-cold-start-note.py` (9 self-tests): the heading's iteration must be **at least** the highest `## Iteration N` in `JOURNAL.md` — `>=` and not equality, because the journal is appended before the heading is retitled and both land in one commit. It fires on **35 of the last 60 commits**, which is not a false-positive rate but a measurement of how long the drift stood. **Fifth instance of *a convention is not a mechanism*, and the first where the convention had already been written down twice.** (`C-0177`.)
- **A STATUS QUALIFIER CAN BE SCOPED AS WELL AS PARTIAL, AND THE THIRD INSTANCE WAS COINED THE SAME DAY THE GATE WENT IN.** After *a closing word about another task* and *`PARTIALLY DONE`*, iteration 41 wrote `**SECOND DELIVERABLE ANSWERED, iteration 41**` on `T-9` — whose three deliverables are done, done and open — and `queue_status` duly read the row CLOSED while `ANSWERS.md` correctly said it was live. The repair is the queue's **established** vocabulary, `**PARTIALLY DONE** — second deliverable answered, iteration 41`, which carries strictly *more* than the coinage: not closed, **and** which part closed. **The rule *a new status word must be tested in BOTH senses the day it is coined* is now a gate rather than a sentence** — and the half that makes it one is not the vocabulary list, which any author can append to, but the **agreement check**: every declared phrase is put through `queue_status` in a synthetic one-row queue and must be read in the sense it is declared in, so declaring the coinage *not closing* fails until `_NOT_CLOSED_QUALIFIER` is taught it too.
- **A GATE IS A CLAIM ABOUT A CORPUS, SO IT IS DISCHARGED BY RUNNING IT — AND A CLAIM THAT *WIRES* A GATE IS NOT OBLIGED TO *RUN* ONE.** `C-0156` promoted `--prose` to a build-failing line in `tools/verify.sh` and the gate was **red at that very commit**: `git archive 49b1a01 | tar -x` and running that tree's own checker exits 1 on **69 tokens in 8 files**. Three facts, no defect in the checker — `./gradlew test` wires only the checker's `--self-test` (the corpus check lives in `verify.sh`, by a convention `build.gradle.kts` argues six times); the claim's Provenance names both mutation tables, 134 self-tests and **no suite run**, and its result file's `gate` record carries `isAGate` and `selfTests` and **no reading**; and its own `F3` row states *"615 tokens in 37 files after the sweep"*, measured by the emitter's `census()`, which **is** the gate's function, reading the **live working tree at emit time** — the unnamed state the same claim's §6 forbids, fixed for the *before* census and not for the *after* one. **The control is `tools/verify.sh --committed`, which exists for exactly this question**, and the cheap rule is that a claim wiring a gate records the gate's reading the way every other claim records a suite result (`C-0158`, `CH-0206`).
- **A MECHANICAL PROSE SWEEP IS BLIND TO FOUR CALL-SITE SHAPES, AND THE LAST OF THEM HAS NO CALL SITE AT ALL.** `T-250` repaired 47 emitters with `x.toString()` → `x.roundedForProse().toString()` and *read* the 80 sentences; **7 tokens in 4 studies survived**, every one a sentence, every one an expression a reader scanning for a bare `${identifier}` skips: a **lambda** inside a template (`${counts.first { it.paths == 30 }.margin}`), a **call** inside one (`${displacements.max()}`), a **`+` concatenation** rather than a template (`" … mu_GC = " + saturatedGouyChapman + " nm"`), and a **hardcoded 16-digit decimal literal** transcribed into a `citedInputs` sentence — which no widening of a rounding helper can reach, because there is nothing to widen. **A residue counted in TOKENS hides that it is two defects**: of 69, **62 were stale artifacts a source repair never re-emitted and 7 were source-side**, and the only thing that separates them is re-emitting first and reading the gate again — the three files whose emitters were never repaired reproduced their committed files **byte for byte**.

- **A MONOTONICITY can be asserted in the same sentence as the four numbers that refute it, and survive, because a claim's prose is read and its own printed numbers are not.** `C-0118` §2: *"More paths are monotonically **worse** there (0.0278431488 → 0.0541089284 → 0.0461988976 → 0.0408747025)"* — one rise and **three falls**. It survived because at that claim's geometry all four cells passed, so the mis-stated law and the true census had the same design consequence; at the corrected geometry **the peak is exactly the region that fails**. No corpus check can reach it — the numbers are the claim's own, they trace clean, and the defect is in the **word between them**. The cheap detector is the one that found it: when a claim names a mechanism, ask whether **two** named effects run opposite ways (here *a dropout is an increase in the attachment pitch* against *a sparse coupling is a net dishing source*), because two opposed effects cannot produce a monotone axis and four points are a census rather than a law.
- **A synthesis drifts by keeping its ANSWERS, not by mis-copying its numbers.** The first end-to-end reconciliation of `ANSWERS.md` found **414 of 415** numeric tokens present in the claim corpus and **three** entries of its *"What we cannot answer, and why"* list that the programme had **answered** — one of them seven iterations earlier. **A deliverable that under-claims is as wrong as one that over-claims and is far harder to catch**, because a reviewer's instinct is to check the assertions and not the disclaimers. Re-read the *"still open"* list against the task queue every time a task closes.
- **A retained checker mechanises the half of the finding that was easy, and the half named as WORST gets left to memory.** `C-0067` concluded that the drift is in the **status** of answers rather than the value of numbers — and the tool it retained in the same hour checks **numbers**. Two iterations later the status class was back: of three places `ANSWERS.md` asserted a task was open, one had been closed by a claim in the previous iteration, with a substantive answer the deliverable was therefore not carrying. **A stale *"`T-129`, open"* contains no number, so no numeric check can ever reach it** — the register that knows is `TASKS.md`. Check it mechanically and run the check **unconditionally**: a check nobody remembers to ask for is not a check, which is the same sentence as the finding it was built from.
- **A GATE'S PREDICATE CAN BE NARROWER THAN THE RULE IT ENFORCES, AND THEN A CLEAN GATE IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE PREDICATE.** `C-0129`'s departure census keys on the leaf `departure` and reads 199 fields in 27 files; the same two records carry three more spellings of the same quantity and the rule's own scope is **601 in 63** — 3.0× in fields, 2.3× in files. `C-0083`'s *a gate that cannot come clean is not a gate* is a statement about a **predicate**, and a predicate can always be narrowed until the tree is clean. The honest form is to wire the gate on what can be made clean and **print the residue beside it, ungated**, with the count and the per-file list; a gate that reports only what it enforces is how a narrow predicate becomes a claim of cleanliness.
- **A hand-audit finds the contradictions that exist WHEN IT RUNS; the very next edit can make a new one, and a STATUS change is the class likeliest to do it.** `C-0080` found `ANSWERS.md` calling `T-45` answered in §1 and unmeasured in §3 — invisible to both existing checks, because one sentence has an owner and the other carries no number. Mechanised as a third check (`C-0088`), it immediately found a **second** task, `T-95`, created **ninety minutes earlier in the same iteration** by a discharge fix that updated one of two mentions. **A one-off reconciliation cannot converge on this class and a standing check can.**
- **A CHECKER'S DEFAULT IS PART OF ITS LOGIC, and the document nobody checks is the one the customer reads.** `ANSWERS.md` carried four retained checkers; `DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md` carried none, and `tools/trace-answers.py --answers DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md` found **3 of its defects running unmodified** — no new logic was ever needed, only a default. `tools/check-corpus-links.py` had the same blind spot in the same week (it scanned `gpd/` only). **Before writing a checker, check whether an existing one is one argument away from the target**; and before trusting a clean run, ask which documents its default actually covers.
- **A *decisions* document's drift has a SIGN, and it is the invisible one.** Every one of `DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md`'s 23 defects **under-claims** — *"nothing has evaluated"*, *"cannot be scoped"*, *"has not been run"* — where `ANSWERS.md`'s six passes found a mixture. It is structural: a document listing what the programme *cannot* do has no sentence that can become false in the adverse direction, and a reviewer's instinct is to check the assertions and not the disclaimers. **A document whose failure mode has a sign must be re-read against the queue every time a task closes**, not audited when somebody remembers.
- **A checker that cannot read `~~strikethrough~~` PENALISES the repair discipline it exists to support.** `C-0071`'s rule is *strike, never delete*, so every correct repair leaves the withdrawn sentence in the file — and `trace-answers.py` read it as a live assertion, so fixing a stale *"`T-191` is open"* by striking it left the flag exactly where it was. Blank struck spans (length- and newline-preserving, or every reported line number is wrong below the first strike) before **every** check, the numeric one included: a withdrawn number is precisely the one a repair should not have to keep traceable. On `ANSWERS.md` that moved 9 tokens `CITED` → `ELSEWHERE` and **0** into `ABSENT`.
- **A challenge can name its own carriers, by filename, and still be unread.** `CH-0125` lists *"`ANSWERS.md` §6 and row (g), `DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md` (twice), `TASKS.md`'s `T-162` and `T-191` rows"*, and `C-0109` says the number *"should not be re-quoted without it"*; both decision-file instances were still unflagged four iterations later. *"A discharge is invisible to whoever files the removal"* is not cured by writing the address down — **grep the corpus for a challenged number when the challenge is filed**, because the carriers are what the challenge is about.
- **Third consecutive iteration in which a check caught the mistake of the person who wrote it** — a headline list (`C-0078`), two `TASKS.md` rows added minutes before (`C-0083`), a half-finished discharge (`C-0088`). That is the argument for writing the check at all, and for running it **unconditionally**: the author is inside the population it is checking.
- **When a drift class cannot be mechanised, say what it would take and price the nearest approximation — do not defer it.** A *superseded* number reads `CITED` **because** it has an owner, so no corpus comparison can see it; the exact check needs a `superseded-by` edge at **statement** granularity that no claim carries, i.e. a corpus convention change. The cheap approximation (flag a number whose newest containing claim is not the cited one) would catch 3 of 5 known instances and is deliberately **not** shipped, because its false-positive rate is unmeasured — and an unmeasured false-positive rate is what makes a checker stop being believed.
- **A CLOSING WORD ABOUT ANOTHER TASK CLOSES THE ROW IT SITS IN, and that failure direction is the unsafe one.** `queue_status` reads the **whole row after the identifier**, so `T-231`'s live cell — *"TODO — LOW, and now unblocked: the honeycomb station lattice was **ANSWERED** by `C-0141`"* — reported **CLOSED**, and `T-111` carries the same defect and is older. `CLAUDE.md` already records that *an unknown status word reads OPEN*, which is safe; this is a **known** word, about a **different** task, inside the row — and it silently removes an open item from the register (57 read where 59 are open). The repair is the queue's own idiom, **verdicts in bold upper case and prose in lower**, so lower-casing the word fixes it and suppressing the check does not (`C-0115`'s rule). ~~The sweep is one pass: any row whose status cell starts `TODO` and which `queue_status` calls CLOSED.~~ **WITHDRAWN, iteration 42 (`C-0177`): that sweep was written to the shape of THIS instance and catches neither the next one nor much else.** It matches **0 of 5** cells of the row that broke next, and over the whole queue it is **21** hits of which **20** are correct — structurally, because `C-0071`'s *strike, never delete* keeps a closed row's original `TODO — MEDIUM …` prose in the file forever, so *containing* `TODO` carries no signal at all. The predicate that works is a **leading, short, bold** run (`tools/check-queue-vocabulary.py`), measured at 8 distinct phrases where *any* bold run gives 29.
- **A STATUS QUALIFIER THAT CONTAINS A CLOSING WORD READS AS CLOSED, AND THAT FAILURE DIRECTION IS THE UNSAFE ONE.** `T-9` closed one of its three deliverables in iteration 35, so its row was written **PARTIALLY DONE** — and `queue_status`, which matches `DONE` on whole words anywhere after the identifier, promptly read the row as CLOSED and dropped a live item from the register (56 open read as 55). This is the twin of *a closing word about ANOTHER task closes the row it sits in*: there the word is about something else, here it is **qualified**, and in both cases an OPEN item silently disappears. The vocabulary rule stands and needs one more clause: **a new status word must be tested in BOTH senses the day it is coined** — that the qualifier does not close, and that it does not stop a bare closing word from closing. `_NOT_CLOSED_QUALIFIER` carries `PARTIALLY` and `PARTLY`, with four tests.
- **A DECISION PUT TO A CUSTOMER CAN BE SUPERSEDED BY A LATER RESULT, AND NOTHING HERE LOOKS.** Decision 7 asks NDI to accept a `38 × 25 nm` tile *"in exchange for removing the last unmeasured dependency"*; corrected to the honeycomb's own two pitches the tile it charges for is **38.08 × 37.504 nm** — essentially §3's square — and the default it offers instead is **38.08 × 56.524 nm**, 1.401× §3's, and no longer flat. **The question reverses.** It is **prose in a numbered list, not a queue row**, so `queue_status` cannot reach it and no retained checker does; it was found by a **token** census, on `38 × 25 nm`. A row-shaped census and a token-shaped census have complementary blind spots and running one is not running the sweep.
- **A claim that SUPPLIES a missing capability does not sweep the notices of its absence** — the other side of *a discharge is invisible to whoever files the removal*. Five claims carry a validity-range item saying this repository has **no** honeycomb station lattice, plan ceiling, placement family or oblique-root price; `C-0141` supplies all four and annotated none of them. Ten occurrences, and they need the same repair as a withdrawn premise in the opposite direction.
