- **A BODY correction makes work CONTINGENT; a LATTICE correction REPRICES it.** `C-0126`'s four-layer line produced **8 contingent** items — questions that might not apply. `C-0141`/`C-0140`, which move the lattice the same body sits on, produced **0 contingent and 11 repriced**, because a geometry correction does not put a question's applicability in doubt, it changes what the answer is worth. **An empty class in a partition is a result**, and it is the shape of the non-collapse rather than the counts that carries it.
- **An annotation on a claim's BODY is not an annotation on its HEADLINE, and the headline is what a reader meets first.** Three claims corrected in iteration 33 carried their correction in a `§`-numbered block and left the title asserting the withdrawn statement — `C-0120`'s still read *"and it costs a third of the footprint"* a whole iteration after its own §2 struck the clause. Annotate the top, and point the body annotation from it.
- **A drift checker's FALSE positives cost more than its true ones, because the tool exists in order to be believed.** Three live traps in this repository's own files: *"Left undone"* contains `DONE` and *"cannot be answered"* upper-cases to `ANSWERED`, so a closing word must be matched **case-sensitively on whole words** (the queue writes verdicts in bold upper case and prose in lower); and *"`T-45`, open since iteration 3, is answered"* is **history**, not an assertion — an `open since|for|from`, or any answering word in the window, cancels it.
- **A number the synthesis got from a RUN rather than from the claim is undetectable by plausibility.** `ANSWERS.md` printed `C-0064`'s four device-range minimaxes as `0.0372 / 0.0436 / 0.0619 / 0.0500` against a claim that says `0.0373 / 0.0435 / 0.0620 / 0.0504` — and the first is a real number this repository produced, `C-0064` recording its optimum as a **manifold** with 0.0372 as the other equally-good member. All four are inside the acceptance convention either way, which is exactly why nothing caught it. **Round a claim's number in the synthesis only where the precision is not the content**: `C-0071`'s three unmargined quantities must be quoted at full width, because `C-0072`'s whole finding is that they are one arithmetic asserted equal to `1e−12`.
- **A range that is a min/max over one claim's own table is stated by no claim, and no `grep` can confirm it.** `"the peak per-load-path force is 3.9–8.9 pN"` is true and is assembled from four cells of `C-0016`; it is exactly the shape of number a synthesis manufactures without noticing it has become a source. Quote the construction beside it, or quote the cells.
- **A number's owner is not the claim cited nearest to it.** `ANSWERS.md`'s working-point layer stiffness sits under a table row citing three claims and belongs to a fourth (`C-0010`'s tangent at 100 pN). Proximity in a synthesis is not provenance.
- **Two claims can be one number, and only the RESULT FILES can see it.** `C-0091` audited six claims said to independently recommend a buffer and found **three**: two were the other three re-read. Each of the six states its route truthfully, so a synthesis reading *claims* is blind to it, and `tools/result-reader-census.py` is too — that finds dependencies flowing through **code**, and a transfer flows through a **person**. `tools/result-transfers.py` finds them; measured recall **2 of 2** against the instances found by hand.
- **An `==` between two emitted numbers is the wrong test for whether they are the same number.** The live instance had one file printing eight significant digits where the other printed nine — equality says *not a transfer*. Compare at the **emission** tolerance and **report the departure** rather than swallowing it.
- **The commoner shape of a transfer is a SUBSET, because a synthesis SELECTS the states it needs.** `T-2` quotes 12 of `T-3`'s 72 solved biases, under the same key name, at departure 0 — invisible to any equal-length comparison. Detecting it costs 260× the exact match (271 s against 1.0 s over 84 files) and returns 7× the output, which is why it is an audit instrument and not a gate.
- **A transfer is not a defect, so it must not be a gate.** It becomes one only where two files are **counted** as independent evidence, and no tool can see that. A gate that fires on a legitimate shared input — a foundation sweep two studies both consume — is turned off within an iteration.
- **A Markdown table row must be ONE physical line, which is the single place [semantic line breaks](https://sembr.org/) do not apply.** `ANSWERS.md`'s §3 row (f) — the longest answer in the primary deliverable — was written across six lines and had not been rendering as a table cell at all. Never break a row at a clause boundary, and check `|`-counts after editing a table.
- **And a bare `|` inside INLINE CODE splits a cell too — GFM splits the row before it parses backticks.** The only correct literal pipe is `\|`, which works inside a code span. A scan of 245 tracked files found **38** defects in **22**, in three kinds: a bare pipe in a body cell, a bare pipe in a **header** (which widens the whole table, so every *correct* body row becomes the odd one out and the writer scanning the rows finds nothing wrong with any of them — this was live in `C-0017`, the claim that owns the coupling mandate), and a genuinely missing cell. `tools/check-markdown-tables.py` checks it, 26 self-tests, exit 1 on any defect.
- **An invariant that forbids fixing something must be taught to the checker, or the checker decays into a warning.** `third-party/` is the problem definition as received and its table must keep its defect, so the sweep excludes it by default and an explicit path still overrides. A check that can never come back clean cannot be a gate — the same design decision as running a check *unconditionally* (`C-0078`): a check's **output discipline** is as much a choice as its logic.
- **`tools/verify.sh` cannot supply a suite test COUNT** — its `trap cleanup EXIT` deletes the snapshot, and `build/test-results` goes with it, so the log carries `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` and nothing to count. Run `./gradlew test -PbuildDirectory=build-<agent>` in the checkout if a claim needs the number; do not guess it from the previous claim's.
- **Before counting corroborating routes, DIFF THEIR RESULT FILES.** Two of the *"six independent routes"* recommending 0.5 mM were the other four read again: `T-2`'s bias clause carries `T-3`'s own blocking bias at **15 of 15** states at a departure of exactly **`0.0`**, and `T-25`'s buffer comparison carries `T-16`'s and `T-4`'s extrema at **20 of 20** at `2.7e−8` — which is one file printing eight significant digits where the other prints nine, so a `==` calls it *not* a transfer. **A synthesis that reads CLAIMS cannot see a transfer, because each claim is telling the truth about itself**; only the JSON shows that two of them are one number. Two independent derivations of the same physics would not agree to nine digits.
- **A buffer advantage — or any "X is better than Y" ratio — is a quantity that needs the state it is read at.** `C-0012`'s *"a factor of five better at 0.5 mM"* is the bias for 100 pN of **blocking** force, i.e. at **zero stroke**; at the held operating point the device occupies it is **1.48–1.57×**, an overstatement of 3.16–3.35×. Same discipline as quoting a stiffness with its compression, applied to a comparison rather than to a value.
- **A verdict that survives can survive on a different reason.** *"The adverse mounting fails, past the 40 pN/nm ceiling"* stayed true after `C-0049` withdrew that ceiling — because `C-0035` had independently shown the same mounting cannot place §3's effort point. Re-check the **ground** of every verdict whose premise is withdrawn, not just the verdict. **And the ground can move in the *favourable* direction and still have to be restated**: three of the four floors under `C-0071`'s knife edge stop firing at `CH-0089`'s measured girth, so *"the best element the catalogue contains and not a buildable design"* survives on a **weaker** ground than the one it was published on — which a synthesis reading only the verdict records as unchanged.
- **A SUPERSEDED STANDING VALUE reads `CITED`, and that is the third drift class.** `C-0067` found numbers in no claim, `C-0078` found tasks asserted open that the queue had closed, and both are now machine-checked at zero — but a number whose *owner still states it* and whose *later supersession lives in a different claim* passes both. Five in one pass (`C-0080`), all headline: `53×` for `68.9×`, a plan **rule**'s 0.2603 for a **search**'s 0.1670, a knife edge quoted against a convention nobody has measured, an *"is still unmeasured"* on a task the file's own §1 calls answered, and a *"requirement"* earned on a load line the recommendation does not use. **The question a tracer cannot ask is *"has a LATER claim superseded this?"*** — ask it per passage, in the range since the last synthesis.
- **The document-side status vocabulary has the OPPOSITE failure direction from the queue-side one, and it is silent.** `tools/trace-answers.py` reads *"absence of a closing word IS open"* on the `TASKS.md` side — safe, because an unknown word reads OPEN. On the **deliverable** side it matches a fixed list (`open|unmeasured|unanswered|unresolved|undetermined|still to do|still missing|not determined`), so a phrasing outside it is not an assertion **at all** and a stale one passes. `DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md`'s *"`T-195` … is the one still `TODO`"* went stale **inside the same iteration that wrote it** and traced clean at 0 contradicted assertions. Add the phrasing the writer actually used, not the one the checker was written for.
- **A tool that checks a document against a corpus cannot see a document that disagrees with ITSELF.** `ANSWERS.md` §1 read *"`T-45` … is answered from published measurement — and the answer is a failure"* while §3 read *"(`T-45` is still unmeasured)"*, two hundred lines apart, **both halves tracing clean**: §1's sentence has an owner and §3's parenthesis carries no number at all, so the numeric half is silent, and *"unmeasured"* is not one of the phrasings the status half looks for. Self-consistency is a separate check, it is cheap because the deliverable is one file, and neither retained tool performs it.
- **A recommendation is priced on an axis, and this programme has exactly ONE column: physics.** *"It costs nothing"* was written of the 0.5 mM buffer on `C-0007`'s ≤ 0.4 % layer sensitivity — a statement about the **modulus** standing in for a statement about the **cost** — and NDI's answer priced it in origami stability at low salt, a fabrication cost with no column here. The distinction that makes this tractable rather than fatal: **the loop CAN see fabrication YIELD wherever it is published** (Rothemund's 63 % → 11 % on linearisation, Ke's 8 bp domains, Strauss's 48–95 % incorporation — which is the entire flatness negative) **and can see fabrication COST nowhere at all**, and no document here distinguishes the two. **Rank what two options BUY, never what they cost, unless a published number prices them — and say which of the two you ranked**, because a reader takes an unqualified recommendation as both.
- **A SUPERSEDED RATIO reads `CITED`, because both of its arguments have owners — and that is a fourth drift class no retained checker reaches.** `ANSWERS.md` and `DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md` both carried *"`10 × 6` is **6.6×** flatter"*, which is `C-0120`'s own number; when `C-0141` moved **both** dishing figures the quotient became **4.06×** and nothing fired, because `tools/trace-answers.py` matches a bare token corpus-wide and `6.6` is cited in five other claims. It stood through four synthesis passes. `C-0080`'s class is a *value* whose owner still states it; this is a *derived* value whose owner still states the **inputs**. **When a synthesis quotes a ratio, quote its construction beside it** — then a moved argument is visible at the point of use.
- **A CORPUS CENSUS IS DATED BY ITS OWN PREMISE SET, and a claim filed the same iteration is invisible to it.** `C-0144`'s 41-entry work list for `T-233` is correct, verified in place and **incomplete**: its five premise families were written from `C-0141`/`C-0140`, and a sibling's `C-0142` re-graded the coupled cells hours later, so **10 further occurrences over 6 lines** — the deliverables' *"1 of 8"* where the truth is **0 of 8** — are in no census at all. Its own validity range names the families as *"a choice"*, which reads as a **recall** caveat; the real caveat is a **date**. A census emits the claim set its families came from, a list published for a downstream editor carries the word **floor**, and the last claim of an iteration re-runs the iteration's own censuses. `CH-0182`, and it is *"a discharge is invisible to whoever files the removal"* with the arrow reversed.
- **A TOKEN CENSUS READS A LINE AND CANNOT SEE THE FRAME THE LINE SITS IN, and here that was a quarter of the work list.** Ten of the same 41 entries lie inside one preserved-verbatim blockquote that `C-0130` filed and *strike, never delete* makes binding — text the repository **forbids** editing, whose whole repair is one superseding banner above it. The discriminator is that every line of the block begins `>`, one regex on an input the tool already has; it takes the published list from **41 to 31**. `CH-0183`. Sister rule: a consumer of a census must inherit its **conventions**, not only its output — a reconciler written for the same list produced false positives by matching `132` inside `C-0132` (the census blanks identifiers length-preservingly) and by firing a honeycomb width family on the **square** lattice's own 112 bp row (the census carries a per-family context requirement). Both were already solved thirty lines away.
- **A SYNTHESIS CLAIM THAT QUOTES A DEFECTIVE TOKEN IN ORDER TO DIAGNOSE IT BECOMES THAT TOKEN'S OWNER**, so a numeric tracer whose corpus contains the audits reports the defect as **traceable**. Partitioned by owner, exactly **3** of `2 097` numeric tokens in the two deliverables have an owner set of synthesis claims only — and **two are live, unqualified assertions in `DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md`, and both are the tokens the two audits were written about** (`42.4` for `C-0032`'s 42.38–61.04, standing **24 iterations**; `0.0344` for `C-0093`'s 0.0344013403). **A repair is applied where the checker REPORTED, and the checker reported one file** — so the surviving instance is always in the sibling document, and the cheap cure is a **cross-document** pass: flag a token live in one deliverable and struck or qualified in the other. And the census **destroys itself** — quoting the two tokens in the challenge gives them a non-synthesis owner, so re-running it returns zero rows. **A census over a corpus that contains the census is not repeatable, and the date it was taken is part of the result.**
- **A CENSUS FAMILY BUILT ON A WITHDRAWAL IS INVALIDATED BY A RESTORATION, and the restored statement is the one that looks most like the withdrawn one.** `T-234`'s `WIDTH` family is *"a honeycomb row length asserted as a uniform tile width"*, from `C-0140`'s withdrawal of `112 bp`; `C-0146` restores `112 bp` as a **row span** — a different functional of the same block — and the family cannot tell them apart, so a correct restatement reads as debt. `CH-0182`'s *a census is dated by its premise set*, third consecutive iteration, and the first where the staleness runs **toward** the withdrawn value rather than away from it.
- **A WIDTH COMPARISON BETWEEN TWO LATTICES CAN BE CROSS-CONVENTION AND LOOK COMPLETE, because the object with no stagger has only ONE width.** `C-0140`'s *"`−1.40 %` beats the square lattice's `−4.80 %`"* reads a honeycomb **bounding box** against a square-lattice **row length**; a uniform raster's two readings coincide, so there is no second figure to notice is missing. Read on the row length both tiles are **112 bp = 38.08 nm** and the advantage is **exactly zero**; read on the bounding box the honeycomb wins by **1.36 nm, which IS the stagger** — it wins by being ragged. And the corpus had already picked a side without saying so: every four-layer plan ceiling, packing verdict and coupled cell outside `C-0146` is computed at **38.08 nm** while both deliverables reported the **footprint** at 39.44.
- **A SPECIFICATION'S TILDE HAS A SCOPE, and this project moved one.** §3's row is *"Tile footprint — 40 × 40 nm (test tiles up to ~70 × 100 nm)"*: the tilde is on the **test tiles**, not on the nominal. This corpus has written *"~40 × 40 nm"* throughout, quietly supplying a tolerance the specification does not state — which is exactly what turns a 3.57 % width ambiguity from a rounding into a question. Quote a specification row **whole** before arguing about its precision.
- **A QUESTION PUT TO THE CUSTOMER CAN CARRY A STALE PRICE, and that is worse than a stale value, because the customer answers the price.** Decision 8 — *"which width is the tile specified to"* — was posed to NDI priced at *"six flat coupled cells of eight against three, through a crossover column decided by a 0.07 nm slack (`CH-0185`)"*, and `C-0148` had **answered `CH-0185` in the same iteration**: the twelfth column belongs to the bounding **box** and to no row. `C-0151` then removed the eleventh too, so the question went out with the only number giving it a **cost dimension** already withdrawn — the one dimension `CLAUDE.md` records this programme has no column for. The existing checks cannot see it: the tracer flags *"`CH-0185` is open"* and not *"worth six cells (`CH-0185`)"*. **A synthesis must re-read the STATUS of every challenge it rests a number on, in the range since the last pass** — `C-0080`'s discipline for task status, which `C-0088` mechanised, applied to the challenge half, where the **prices** live.
- **A question whose admissible answers you enumerate cannot return the one you did not enumerate, and the missing shape is a PRICE.** `DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md` declared *"a one-sentence answer is enough for all six; three of them need a single word"* and offered decisions 1, 2 and 6 as yes/no or adopt/hold. **All six were answered and three came back in a shape the document did not offer** — a price, a contingency on another decision, and an answer volunteered inside a *different* question — and they are exactly the three with a cost dimension. The three it predicted correctly (a material, a device count, a scaffold) have none. So a specification question must leave the price line explicitly open — *"and if this costs something, what?"* — or its answer shape will be wrong in precisely the cases where the answer matters.

- **A RE-EMISSION SWEEP IS A TOPOLOGICAL SORT OF THE READER CENSUS, NOT A LIST.** `C-0101` established the *"re-emit the downstream file and amend the claim"* discipline and then ran a **consumer before its own producer** inside its own eleven-file sweep, so the committed `T-157` reproduced the *pre-repair* `T-149` and `C-0092`'s `A5` clause measured a difference already absorbed — 1.0000–3.3380× where the truth is **1.0000 at every fold** (`CH-0131`, `C-0117`). `tools/result-reader-census.py` already derives the graph such a sort needs, **including the transitive edges a grep cannot see**, so the cure was twenty lines: `tools/reemission-order.py`. **Ask it before any sweep.** And the general gate is priced and declined: **499** nonzero reproduction departures across **64 of 104** result files, most of them legitimate literature cross-checks, so a residual-based staleness gate would fire constantly on correct files.
- **A PROOF that a shared-source change is invisible is not a substitute for RUNNING the consumers, because the run also checks everything the proof was not about.** The `PullInStability.kt` clamp genuinely moved nothing — and re-running its five consumers anyway is what exposed the stale `T-157` above. One question from a coordinator, one defect. The cheap check that a consumer is *not* stale needs no solve at all: compare the named values it echoes against the producer's current file (`T-138`←`T-136`, clean, six of eight values matched exactly).
- **`kotlin.daemon.jvmargs=-Xmx3g` is a PER-DAEMON cure and the constraint is the PRODUCT.** Four concurrent agents each running a `verify` and a `study` want 4 × 3 GB on a 15 GB box: measured at load 11.3, 3 GB available, two daemons at 4.1 and 3.3 GB RSS, and an `OOMErrorException` **with the documented flag already in `gradle.properties`**. Gradle falls back to in-process and the work completes, half an hour late. **Stagger the heavy runs; do not raise the per-daemon ceiling**, which makes the product worse. Three agents is this box's ceiling for compiling work — four is fine for *filing* it.
- **A transient API failure costs nothing if the agent has written nothing, and everything if it has.** Six subagent launches died on 529s in one iteration here; the tree was clean after every one, so no reserved number was consumed and no work was lost — and the right response after two failures is to **stop relaunching and do the work in the main context**, which is where all three of that iteration's tasks were actually completed. Tell a subagent to write its task file early, so a mid-run failure leaves something to resume from.

- **A STATION CENSUS IS SET BY THE FACE, NOT BY THE BODY, so a THICKER block offers FEWER attachment sites.** A buried helix has all three honeycomb azimuths occupied by neighbours and therefore no free direction to root on. At a fixed 60 helices, `15 × 4` has 15 top-face helices and `10 × 6` has 10 — so the flatter, stiffer, threshold-free cross-section supplies **60** stations against **90**. **A thicker tile buys rigidity and spends attachment lattice**, which is the opposite of the intuition and the axis on which a coupling runs out of room.
- **The honeycomb's 7 bp and 21 bp are in the same sentence of the same paper and they are not interchangeable.** *"potential staple-crossover positions occur every seven base pairs"* is **any** azimuth's next position; *"which repeat every 21 base pairs"* is **one** azimuth's period. An attachment roots on one azimuth, so its ladder is the **21** — a factor of three, and the square lattice's analogous pair (8 bp planes, the same pair every 32 bp) does not transfer at all.
- **A HONEYCOMB HAS NO STRAIGHT CHAIN, so every x-raster row is corrugated and its neighbour-class difference ALTERNATES.** From a sublattice-A site the bonds are `90/210/330°` and from a B site `270/30/150°`; no offset appears in both lists, so no path of three helices is straight. Douglas et al. state the same of their own blocks — *"the x-raster rows … are **corrugated**; they stagger up and down and encompass helices that are actually at two different y-positions"* — and it means consecutive helices of a row sit on **opposite sublattices**. It also forces an **even** number of helices per row (both row ends must carry the *downward* vertical bond, and that bond points up on one sublattice and down on the other): all seven folded designs have `n = 4, 6, 8, 10, 16, 20, 30`.
- **AND BECAUSE THE FACE IS CORRUGATED, ITS ROOTING HELICES SIT AT GAPS OF `d` AND `2d` ABOUT THE `3d/2` LADDER, so an abstract attachment grid lands `d/4` = 0.634 nm off its own helix, ALTERNATING in sign across the rows.** That is this file's own first-order symmetry break — *alternating an attachment's STATION across the helices is the same symmetry break as alternating its STIFFNESS* — except that on a honeycomb it is not a design choice but what the lattice hands you. Measured, snapping each station onto its rooting helix is worth **0.9606–1.0826×**, and **its sign is set by the distribution**: adverse at every equal-spring placement and both ways at the rim-graded ones.
- **TWO SIGN ALTERNATIONS CANCEL ONLY WHILE THEY SHARE A PHASE, AND A BOUNDARY THAT RESETS ONE OF THEM DESTROYS THE CANCELLATION — SO COUNT THE BOUNDARIES.** On a honeycomb x-raster the cross-section's `Δ_geom` and the scaffold's axial direction `s` both alternate, and `Δ_eff = s·Δ_geom` is **constant along a row interior** — consecutive rows carrying opposite senses. Each of the `m − 1` **row turns** breaks the phase and contaminates three helices, so a `15 × 4` block ends at **30 against 28** and a **one-row** raster is the only constant case. `CLAUDE.md`'s own *"two independent sign alternations can CANCEL"* is a statement about **one** period; composing them is still the discipline, and the new question it raises is *how many times is the composition reset*.
- **A LATTICE RULE TRANSFERS BETWEEN LATTICES ONLY IF ITS CLASS DIFFERENCE IS SELF-INVERSE IN THE NEW MODULUS.** `C-0086`'s square-lattice width rule is unconditional because the sheet's two in-plane neighbours are 180° apart — **two** azimuth classes — and `2` is its own negative modulo 4, so the traversal-direction alternation cannot reach it. Modulo 3 neither 1 nor 2 is self-inverse, which is the whole reason the honeycomb's `N ≡ 7Δ + {0,10,11} (mod 21)` needs a **turn sense** and the square sheet's does not. One line of arithmetic, and it predicts which lattice rules are conditional before any of them is derived.
- **A RESIDUE CONDITION ON CROSSOVERS DOES NOT BIND A TURN MADE OF UNPAIRED SCAFFOLD — count the paper's own per-helix nucleotide allotment before applying one.** Douglas et al.'s blocks allot **126 nt per helix, 98 paired and 28 as front and rear unpaired loops**, so every raster turn passes through 28 single-stranded nucleotides and no crossover lattice constrains it. That is how a block whose turn sense alternates is folded with all sixty helices at one length — and `60 × 126 = 7 560`, `64 × 126 = 8 064`, **both exact**, which also identifies which scaffold each design used. A budget written on the paired length alone has no line for the turns: 1 680 nt on a 60-helix tile, against M13's 529 spare at 112 bp.
- **A honeycomb cross-section has TWO pitches and only their PRODUCT is the cell: `3d/2` in plane, `d√3/2` through the thickness, `3√3/4 · d² = 8.354 nm²` per helix.** Writing the array as `d × d` — which every four-layer claim in this repository does — packs it **`3√3/4` = 1.299×** tighter than any honeycomb of that bond length can be, and **correcting the layer spacing ALONE makes the density error worse**, 1.50× against 1.30×: this file's own standing note (*"a honeycomb array stacks its rows at `d√3/2`"*) is one of the two factors and they travel together. The consequence is not a rounding: every `edgeY` is exactly **1.5×** too small, so a 15-row block is **56.5 nm** across and not 38.0, and because `10 × 1.5 = 15` the corrected `10 × 6` carries the very `edgeY` the corpus gave `15 × 4` — **`C-0120`'s footprint ordering reverses** and `C-0116`'s coupling threshold on `15 × 4` moves *into* the measured 0.26–0.33 band (`CH-0174`).
- **An `m × n` honeycomb block is `m` CORRUGATED x-raster rows of `n` helices, and the paper says so in one sentence** — *"the x-raster rows … are corrugated; they stagger up and down and encompass helices that are actually at two different y-positions"*. So `m` is the tile's **in-plane** count at pitch `3d/2` and `n` its **thickness** count at pitch `d√3/2`; the face normal to the thin direction carries **one helix per x-raster row**, which is why the census is `m` and not `m·n`.
- **The 7 bp stagger between adjacent station rows on a honeycomb face is FORCED, and the ROOTING-HELIX PARITY then decides whether a centro-symmetric placement family exists at all.** The two face sublattices carry their free azimuth on two *different* bond classes, whose crossover residues differ by 7 or 14 bp mod 21 — never 0 — so no honeycomb face has its station rows in register. Rows `r` and `m − 1 − r` share a parity when `m` is **odd**, so the reflection demands the *same* ladder phase and the stagger refuses it: `15 × 4` admits **no** centro-symmetric lattice at any of 21 phases, either offset, either row length. When `m` is **even** the reflection *swaps* the phases and the stagger is what makes the symmetry available: `10 × 6` admits one at the **full** station count. On the square lattice an 8 bp stagger is a first-order symmetry break a design must *choose*; here it cannot be designed out.
- **A denser station ladder is a LARGER CHOICE SET, not a tighter plan budget, and the inboard bound binds only at SATURATION.** The honeycomb's 21 bp ladder gives a collinear budget of `7.14 − 2.536 = 4.604 nm` against the square lattice's 8.19, which predicts a tighter ceiling and is wrong below saturation: a placement that spends 45 of 90 stations **skips** stations, so the binding pitch is 42 bp and the exact bisection returns **9.52 nm**. Only at 90 of 90 does it fall to 2.38 nm — and there the tile **edge** binds, not the neighbour. Eleventh instance of *quote it with the state it is read at*, and the state is the **occupancy**.
- **The placement machinery in this repository is LATTICE-GENERIC and the site GENERATOR is not.** `maximumPlanCeilingForCount`, `latticeRootCapacity`, `maximumRootedElementsInRow`, `rootedLengthCeiling`, `rowRootOptions`, `armDirections` and `centroSymmetricPlacementsOn` all take an explicit `List<List<Double>>` and took the honeycomb lattice **unmodified**, reproducing `C-0072`'s 9.535 nm on the square lattice through the same call. What is square-lattice-specific is `upwardRootLattice`/`upwardHingeSites` (four azimuths, 8 bp planes, `k ≡ 2r+3 (mod 4)`, 32 bp pitch), `centroSymmetricUpwardPhases` (a congruence that assumes `r` and `D−1−r` share a sublattice), `CrossoverLayout` (period 32, two parities) and the grillage's single pitch. **Before writing a honeycomb placement search, check whether the existing one is one argument away.**

## Anti-patterns to avoid

- Do not add content to this file that is already discoverable by reading the source or build scripts — that inflates context without adding signal, reducing AI agent task success rates (see [arxiv 2602.11988](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11988)).
